Appearance
Shri Mateen Siddiqui, Shri Awadh Tripathi, Smt. Hamida Siddiqui, Shri H.S. Patel,
Shri R.S. Patel, Shri Abhishek Sinha, Shri Parag Kotecha, Shri B.D. Guru, Shri D.R.
Patel, Shri V.K.Pandey, Shri Jitendra Shrivastava, Shri R.S. Baghel, Shri Manoj Kumar
Jaiswal, Shri Sanjay Patel, Shri CJK Rao, Shri Anchal Kharya, Shri R.K.Sharma,
Shri K.K. Patel, Shri Somkant Verma, Shri Chandresh Shrivastava, Shri A.K. Yadav,
Shri Roop Naik, Shri Ajay Shrivastava, Ms. Meha Kumar, Shri Rakesh Thakur and
Shri Govind Dewangan, counsel for the respective petitioners.
Shri Prafull Bharat, Additional Advocate General with Shri R.K. Gupta, Dy.A.G.,
Shri Ayaz Naved, Shri Sushobhit Singh and Ms. Pushpa Dwivedi, counsel for the
respective respondents.
 S.B.:Hon'ble Shri Justice Manindra Mohan Shrivastava
CAV Order
 23/12/2016
1. In this batch of petitions, Assistant Teachers, who are posted and working in
various schools in the jurisdiction of Raigarh Jila Panchayat, Kanker Jila
Panchayat, Dhamtari and Ambikapur Jila Panchayat, have assailed legality and
validity of transfers made on large scale by the Chief Executive Officer of Jila
Panchayat Raigarh, Jila Panchayat, Kanker and Jila Panchayat, Ambikapur as
also by the Collector of Dhamtari. By these transfer orders, the petitioners,

-305-
Assistant Teachers, in these petitions who were working in schools within the
jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat have been transferred to schools within the
jurisdiction of another Jila Panchayat.
2. Learned counsel appearing for the petitioners in these petitions firstly raised a
common ground to challenge the transfer order that the present statutory scheme
of Chhattisgarh Panchayat Raj Adhiniyam, 1993 (hereinafter referred to as “the
Act of 1993”), read along with the Chhattisgarh Panchayat Service (Recruitment
and General Conditions of Services) Rules, 1999 (hereinafter referred to as “the
Rules of 1999”) and Chhattisgarh Teacher (Panchayat) Cadre (Recruitment and
Conditions of Service) Rules, 2012 (hereinafter referred to as “the Rules of 2012”)
do not empower any of the authority who has passed the impugned order, to
transfer Assistant Teachers outside the jurisdiction of their parent Jila Panchayat.
In addition to raising a common ground of challenge to all the transfer orders as
above, it has been argued that for the purpose of identifying surplus teachers of
one school and to transfer them to other school where there is lack of teachers,
old and stale data of 2014 has been made a basis and no latest data prior to date
of transfers were collected. Further submission is that the transfer orders have
been made without considering the Assistant Teachers for promotion to the next
higher post of Teachers under the Rules of 2012 against the vacancy of Teachers
available within the jurisdiction of the Jila Panchayat where these petitioners are
posted. It has also been submitted that in many cases, recipient Jila Panchayat
where the services have been transferred are not prepared to accept teachers of
Arts subject sent from other Jila Panchayat by referring to letter dated 16.8.2016
filed as Annexure AD/4 in WPS No. 1273 of 2016. In some cases, argument has
been advanced that where appointments have been made on compassionate
basis, those Assistant Teachers could not be subjected to transfer. Further
submission is that as the object and purpose of rationalization is to rationalize the
teachers on the basis of Pupil-Teacher ratio only at the primary level, therefore, for
this additional reason, transfer could not be made outside the respective
jurisdiction of the Jila Panchayat in which the petitioners are posted. In some of
the cases, transfer order has been challenged on the ground that those teachers
who are office bearers are entitled to be exemption from transfer on the ground
that they are Office Bearers. In many cases, transfer order is also challenged on
the ground that while effecting transfer, the policy of keeping husband and wife

-306-
together at the same or nearby stations has not been kept in view and the effect of
transfer is that now the husband and wife are going to be posted in school under
the jurisdiction of different Jila Panchayats. It has also been argued that as per
provisions contained in Scheduled-II of the Rules of 2012, the appointing authority
is General Administration Committee of Jila Panchayat, therefore, the transfer
could be effected only on the basis of resolution passed by the General
Administration Committee of Jila Panchayat and the Chief Executive Officer or the
Collector had no jurisdiction to transfer Assistant Teachers from one place to other
place. In many cases, argument has been advanced that while effecting transfers,
rationalization policy and the parameters for identifying surplus teachers have not
been followed. It has also been argued that under the policy dated 27.5.2014,
entire exercise of transfer was required to be completed by the end of June, 2014,
therefore, the exercise undertaken by various Jila Panchayats or the Collector to
effect transfer on the basis of policy dated 27.5.2014 is rendered illegal as those
transfers have been effected after June, 2014. It has also been argued that
women and disabled teachers were entitled to exemption, yet they have been
subjected to transfer. It has also been contended that the executive instructions
cannot override the statutory provisions contained in Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999
and in the absence of there being any power available with the authority to
transfer outside the jurisdiction of Jila Panchayat, the exercise of transfer is bad in
Law. In some of the cases where the transfer orders were issued prior to
11.12.2015, it has been argued that even prior to amendment in Rule 27 of the
Rules of 1999, it is only those teachers who are working with Jila Panchayat,
which does not include Assistant Teachers appointed by the Janpad Panchayat
level, who could be transferred from jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to other Jila
Panchayat by the Director of Jila Panchayat and in any case, the power only
rested with the Director, Panchayat at the State level. Therefore, it is contended
that the Chief Executive Officer of Jila Panchayat or the Collector had no
jurisdiction to transfer the Assistant Teachers from jurisdiction of one Jila
Panchayat to other Jila Panchayat.
3. On the other hand, learned counsel for the State and counsel for the Jila
Panchayat Raigarh, Dhamtari and Ambikapur have submitted that the transfers
have been effected to comply with the statutory norms relating to Pupil-Teacher
Ratio as prescribed under the Right of Children to Free and Compulsory

-307-
Education Act, 2009 (hereinafter referred to as “RTE Act”) and the Right of
Children to Free and Compulsory Education Rules, 2010 (hereinafter referred to
as “RTE Rules”). The rationalization policy was laid down by the State
Government with the aim and object of providing teachers in all the schools in a
manner that the Pupil-Teacher Ratio is achieved as per the statutory requirements
of RTE Act and RTE Rules. Learned counsel for the State and counsel for the
aforesaid Jila Panchayats argued that the provision contained in Section 8, 19 and
25 of the RTE Act made it imperative to undertake the exercise of posting
teachers in various schools in different Jila Panchayat. Therefore, the exercise is
only intended to ensure effective implementation of the provision of RTE Act and
RTE Rules. Learned counsel for the State argued that directions were issued by
the Supreme Court while deciding PIL in the case of Environmental and
Consumer Protection Foundation Vs. Delhi Administration and Ors. (2012)
10 SCC 197 and in order to fulfill those directives, it became necessary to achieve
Pupil-Teacher ratio as provided in Rule 22 of the RTE Rules. According to learned
State counsel, Section 35 of the RTE Act empowers the Government to issue
directions to local authority to implement the provision of the RTE Act. The
Government from time to time, issued various policies of rationalization of posting
of teachers with an object to ensure that the teachers are so posted in the schools
that the Pupil-Teacher ratio in all the schools is achieved. Reliance has been
placed on the decision of the Supreme Court in the case of Joint Action
Committee of Air Line Pilots' Association of India (ALPAI) and Ors. Vs.
Director General of Civil Aviation and Ors.(2011) 5 SCC 435. Learned counsel
for the respondents further argued that in so far as transfers which have been
effected prior to amendment in Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999 i.e. prior to
11.12.2015, the Assistant Teachers could be transferred from jurisdiction of one
Jila Panchayat to other Jila Panchayat and under the policy directives of the State
Govt., the power was conferred on respective Chief Executive Officers of Jila
Panchayat and the Collector.
4. While in batch of petitions, transfer orders have been challenged on more than
one grounds, one common ground has been taken in all the petitions is that the
transfer orders have been passed transferring the Assistant Teachers from the
jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to other Jila Panchayat which is not permissible
under the scheme of the Panchayat Raj Adhiniyam and Rules framed thereunder.

-308-
In order to appreciate the submissions made by learned counsel for the respective
parties on the issue of competence and jurisdiction of the Chief Executive Officer
of respective Jila Panchayat and Collector to transfer Assistant Teachers from
jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to jurisdiction of another Jila Panchayat, it would
be relevant to examine the statutory scheme of the Act of 1993 and various Rules
framed thereunder governing terms and conditions of service of Assistant
Teachers.
5. There is no dispute with regard to the fact that the petitioners are all Assistant
Teachers who have been appointed in various schools run under the control of
Janpad Panchayats and ultimate control of Jila Panchayat. All these Assistant
Teachers have been appointed by Panchayat Authority in exercise of powers
conferred under the Rules of 2012. The aforesaid Rules provide for constitution of
service, classification and scale of pay, method of recruitment, compassionate
appointment, probation, promotion, discipline & control, termination of service of
teachers in three different categories known as Assistant Teacher (Panchayat),
Teacher (Panchayat) and Lecturer (Panchayat) as specified in Schedule-I
appended to the Rules of 2012. The petitioners who are Assistant Teachers are
borne in the cadre of Assistant Teachers in the services of Panchayat constituted
under Rule 4 of the Rules of 2012. Rule 12 of the Rules of 2012 provides for
general conditions of service which is relevant and extracted hereinbelow:
“12. General Conditions of Service-Conditions of
service other than mentioned above shall be the same as
applicable to other employees of Jila Panchayat or Janpad
Panchayat, as the case may be.”
6. From the aforesaid provision, it is clear that conditions of service other than those
mentioned in Rules of 2012 shall be the same as applicable to other employees of
Jila Panchayat or Janpad Panchayat as the case may be.
7. In exercise of powers conferred under Section 70 read with Section 95 of the Act
of 1993, Rules having statutory force, have been framed, governing terms and
conditions of Panchayat employees known as Chhattisgarh Panchayat Service
(Recruitment and General Conditions of Services) Rules, 1999. These are the
Rules of general applications which govern the terms and conditions of services of

-309-
Assistant Teachers by virtue of provision contained in Rule 12 of the Rules of
2012 referred to hereinabove. Therefore, where there are no specific Rules
governing terms and conditions of service of Assistant Teachers in the Rules of
2012, the general provisions contained in the Rules of 1999 would be applicable
to the Assistant Teachers. Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999 importantly provides for
appointment of Panchayat employees and the same is reproduced herein-below
for ready reference:
“27. Appointment of Panchayat employ to and his
Panchayat:- Notwithstanding anything contained in these
rules, the appointing authority on application made by any
employee of Panchayat Service of other Panchayat, may
appoint such employee to a same or equivalent post in the
Panchayat service on such terms and conditions as may be
mutually agreed upon between the two Panchayats, and
subject to the following conditions, namely:-
(i) Such appointment shall not be made to post to be
filled by promotion;
(ii) The person so appointed shall receive the junior
most rank, for the purpose of seniority, in the cadre of the
post to which he is appointed, as it stands on the date of
such appointment.”
8. Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999 inter alia provides that the appointing authority on
application made by any employee of Panchayat service of other Panchayat, may
appoint such employee to a same or equivalent post in the Panchayat service on
such terms and conditions as may be mutually agreed upon between the two
Panchayats subject to the conditions enumerated in clauses (i) and (ii) thereof.
According to aforesaid Rule, an Assistant Teacher appointed within the
jurisdiction of one Panchayat may be transferred to another Panchayat service
only on application made by the Assistant Teacher on terms and conditions which
may be mutually agreed upon between two Panchayats.
9. Vide Notification dated 30th November, 2012, published in Chhattisgarh Rajpatra

-310-
dated 30th November, 2012, Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999 was amended by the
State Govt. in exercise of statutory powers adding two proviso providing for
transfer of Panchayat employees from one Janpad Panchayat to other Janpad
Panchayat and from one Jila Panchayat to other Jila Panchayat. The two proviso
which were added by way of amendments were as below :
“Provided that in the public interest the Chief Executive
Officer of concerned Jila Panchayat may transfer any
employee from one Janpad Panchayat to another Janpad
Panchayat within the district and in case of such transfer, the
seniority of the transferred employee shall not be affected.
Provided further that in the public interest the
Commissioner cum Director, Panchayat may transfer any
employee of Panchayat services, working with the Jila
Panchayat, from one Jila Panchayat to another Jila
Panchayat in the State and in case of such transfer, the
seniority of the transferred employee shall not be affected”
10. The aforesaid two proviso were, however, omitted by a subsequent amendment in
the Rules vide Notification dated 1st December, 2015, published in Chhattisgarh
Rajpatra dated 11th December, 2015. The Notification being extremely relevant for
decision of this case is reproduced as below:
“ Naya Raipur, the 1st December, 2015”
No.P-1141/PGVV/22/2015/786- In exercise of the powers
conferred by sub-section (2) of Section 70 of the Chhattisgarh
Panchayat Raj Adhiniyam, 1993 (No.1 of 1994), the State
Government, hereby, makes the following further amendment
in the Chhattisgarh Panchayat Service (Recruitment and
General Conditions of Services) Rules, 1999, the same having
been previously published as required by sub-section (3) of
Section 95 of the said Act, namely :-
Amendment
In the said rules-1. In clause (ii) of Rule 27, for the colon “;”, after the word
“appointment”, the full stop “.” shall be substituted.

-311-
2. First and second proviso of clause (ii) of Rule 27 shall be
omitted.
 By order and in the name of the Governor of Chhattisgarh
S.L. Nayak, Joint Secretary”
Therefore, with effect from 11th December, 2015, the first and second
proviso of clause (ii) of Rule 27 of the Rules of 2012 were omitted.
It would thus be clear from the above legislative history that initially when
the Rules of 1999 were framed, Rule 27 thereof contemplated transfer of
Panchayat employee from one Panchayat to other Panchayat only on application
made by the employee and later on, by addition of two proviso, the Rule permitted
transfer of Panchayat employees from one Janpad Panchayat to another Janpad
Panchayat in the same district and transfer of Panchayat employee from one Jila
Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat in the State. The aforesaid two proviso
having been omitted subsequently, the earlier legal position under the Rule 27 of
the Rules of 1999 was restored meaning thereby that a Panchayat employee
could be transferred from one Panchayat to other Panchayat only on an
application made by the employee and not otherwise. The omission of first and
second proviso of clause (ii) of Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999 clearly manifests the
intention of the Rule Making Authority that involuntarily transfer of the Panchayat
employees which included Assistant Teachers as well, would not be permissible
from one Panchayat to the other Panchayat except where the employee himself
applies for his transfer to other Panchayat, in which case, the two Panchayats
may enter into mutual agreement with regard to terms and conditions of such
transfer.
11. Even when the two proviso existed in the statute book, until omitted vide
Notification dated 1st December, 2015 published on 11th December, 2015, an
employee of one Janpad Panchayat could be transferred to other Janpad
Panchayat within the district in public interest, by the Chief Executive Officer of
concerned Jila Panchayat and transfer from jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to
other Jila Panchayat in the State could be made in public interest by the
Commissioner cum Director Panchayat. Therefore, even when the aforesaid two
proviso were in force, transfer from one Jila Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat
could be made only by the Commissioner cum Director Panchayat, who is the

-312-
controlling authority of Panchayat Institution in the State.
12.Irresistible conclusion, therefore, is that ever-since promulgation of the Rules of
1999, before and after amendment made, as referred to herein-above, power was
never conferred on the Chief Executive Officer of the Jila Panchayat or the
Collector of a district within which a particular Jila Panchayat functions, to transfer
a Panchayat employee including Assistant Teacher from the jurisdiction of one Jila
Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat in the State. Even under the Rules of 2012,
there is no provision which confers power on the Chief Executive Officer of Jila
Panchayat or the Collector of the district to transfer an Assistant Teacher from the
jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat in the State.
13.Once the terms and conditions of Assistant Teacher are governed by the provision
contained in the Act of 1993, Rules of 1999 and Rules of 2012, it was not
permissible to transfer an Assistant Teacher from the jurisdiction of one Jila
Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat except in the manner provided under the Act
and Rules.
14.During the course of arguments, learned counsel for the State and counsel for
various Jila Panchayats sought to justify the action on the basis of various
circulars issued by the State from time to time which have been filed in WPS No.
1273 of 2016 as Annexure D-1 (circular dated 27.5.2014), Annexure D-2 (circular
dated 31.5.2014), Annexure D-3 (circular dated 2.7.2014), Annexure D-4 (circular
dated 11.7.2014), Annexure D-5 (circular dated 1.9.2014), Annexure D-6 (circular
dated 21.7.2015, Annexure D-7 (order dated 9.9.2015) and Annexure D-8 (order
dated 15.10.2015). All these circulars and orders are merely executive and
administrative in nature. The circulars/orders have been issued by the School
Education Department and by Panchayat and Rural Development Department.
These circulars/orders provide for rationalization of teachers in various schools
run by Panchayat and School Education Department. In the absence of there
being any statutory backing, these circulars/orders would not confer any authority
or power on the Chief Executive Officer of Jila Panchayat or the Collector of the
district to transfer the Assistant Teachers from the jurisdiction of one Jila
Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat. In fact, these circulars and orders mainly
provide for the scheme of rationalization intended to provide teachers in the
schools according to the need of teachers.

-313-
Learned counsel for the respondents also sought to justify the action on the
basis that as the provision contained in the RTE Act and RTE Rules mandated
maintenance of Pupil -Teacher ratio prescribed under the Law, it had become
necessary to transfer Assistant Teachers from the schools of one Janpad
Panchayat to other Janpad Panchayat even from one Jila Panchayat to other Jila
Panchayat. This contention is liable to be rejected because to fulfill the object of
maintaining Pupil-Teacher ratio under the provisions of RTE Act and RTE Rules,
the Assistant Teachers could not be transferred from one Jila Panchayat to other
Jila Panchayat contrary to the provisions contained in the statutory Rules
governing terms and conditions of the Assistant Teachers. Once the statutory
Rules governing the terms and conditions did not permit involuntary transfer of
Assistant Teachers under any circumstance, from one Panchayat to another
Panchayat, except on application made by the concerned Assistant Teacher, as
provided in Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999, the action could not be justified in Law. It
is well settled legal proposition that lawful ends can be achieved only by following
lawful means.
15.In order to comply with the statutory requirement of maintaining Pupil-Teacher
ratio as mandated under RTE Act and RTE Rules, the respondents were obliged
under the law to adopt only lawful means to effect transfer of teachers. For
reasons best known to the respondent State, the two proviso added by way of
amendment in Rule 27 of the Rules of 1999 were omitted vide Notification dated
1.12.2015 published on 11th December, 2015. This Court fails to understand, if at
all, the State Government intended to effect transfer of Assistant Teachers from
jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat to maintain Pupil-Teacher ratio required under the RTE Act and RTE Rules, why the two proviso
under Rule 27 of the Act of 1999, one of which permitted transfer from one Jila
Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat, was omitted.
16.Be that as it may, in the absence of there being any statutory power conferred on
the Chief Executive Officer of Jila Panchayat or the Collector of the district to
transfer an Assistant Teacher from jurisdiction of one Jila Panchayat to other Jila
Panchayat, no amount of justification for such transfer would validate the action
and the order of transfer which is clearly without jurisdiction and authority of Law.
17.There are many cases before this Court where transfer were effected prior to

-314-
publication of Notification dated 1.12.2015. In that regard it has to be noted that
under the second proviso of clause (ii) of Rule 27, as it existed prior to its
omission, power of transfer of Assistant Teacher from one Jila Panchayat to
another Jila Panchayat rested only with the Commissioner cum Director
Panchayat and not with the Chief Executive Officer of Jila Panchayat or the
Collector of the district. Therefore, all the transfer orders in the present cases
which have been issued prior to or after 11.12.2015 by the Chief Executive Officer
of various Jila Panchayats and Collector of the District are illegal and without the
authority of Law. Under the existing statutory scheme as engrafted under Rule 27
of the Rules of 1999, as it stood on the date of issuing impugned transfer order, an
Assistant Teacher could be transferred from one Jila Panchayat to other Jila
Panchayat only on the application made by the Assistant Teacher and not
otherwise. Rule only permits voluntarily transfer from one Jila Panchayat to
another Jila Panchayat and Rule nowhere permits involuntarily transfer of
Assistant Teacher from one Jila Panchayat to other Jila Panchayat.
18.The administrative instructions issued from time to time referred to herein-above
as Annexure D-1 to Annexure D-8, being in violation of statutory Rules to the
extent it directed the Collector and the Chief Executive Officer of Jila Panchayat to
transfer Assistant Teacher from one Jila Panchayat to another Jila Panchayat,
would not provide valid justification for transfer. These administrative instructions
could not override the statutorily Rules.
19.In the case of State of Orissa and Ors. Vs. Prasana Kumar Sahoo (AIR 2007
SC 2588), it was held as under:
“12. Even a policy decision taken by the State in
exercise of its jurisdiction under Article 162 of the
Constitution of India would be subservient to the
recruitment rules framed by the State either in terms
of a legislative Act or the proviso appended to Article
309 of the Constitution of India. A purported policy
decision issued by way of an executive instruction
cannot override the statute or statutory rules far less
the constitutional provisions.”

-315-
In the case of Punjab Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. Ranjodh
Singh (2007) 2 SCC 491, it was held thus:
“10. A statutory board is an autonomous body.
Nothing has been brought to our notice to show that
under the statute any direction issued by the State
shall be binding on it. The State may have some
control with regard to recruitment of employees of
local authorities, but such control must be exercised
by the State strictly in terms of the provisions of the
Act. The statutory bodies are bound to apply the
rules of recruitment laid down under the statutory
rules. They being “State” within the meaning of
Article 12 of the Constitution of India, are bound to
implement the constitutional scheme of equality.
Neither can the statutory bodies refuse to fulfill such
constitutional duty, nor can the State issue any
direction contrary to or inconsistent with the
constitutional principles adumbrated under Articles
14 and 16 of the Constitution of India. The purported
directions of the State were otherwise bad in law
insofar as thereby the statutory rules were sought to
be superseded. A circular letter furthermore is not a
statutory instrument. It was not even issued by the
State in exercise of the power under Article 162 of
the Constitution of India. Even a scheme issued
under Article 162 of the Constitution of India, would
not prevail over statutory rules.”
In yet another decision in the case of Union of India Vs. Central
Electrical & Mechanical Engg. Service (Ce & Mes) Group 'A' (direct
Recruits) Assn. CPWD (2008) 1 SC 354, it was held as under:
“9. The aforementioned office orders dated 1-8-2002
and 11-3-2003 are not statutory in character. They
even ex facie do not satisfy the requirements of Arti-

-316-
cle 162 of the Constitution of India. Indisputably, the
disciplines of Civil, Electrical and Mechanical in the
Central Public Works Department are different and
distinct. The said office orders provided that disci-plines referred to therein including Civil and Electrical
were to work under the control of the zonal head be-ing either a Chief Engineer (Civil) or Chief Engineer
(Electrical). It has not been denied or disputed that
the post of Chief Engineer (Electrical or Civil), was
beyond the purview of the Rules. It is beyond any
cavil that there are posts of Chief Engineer in all the
four wings of the Central Public Works Department.
The Rules provide for posts of Civil Engineers. As by
reason of the impugned orders, some sort of amalga-mation of different cadres are sought to be made be-yond the legal sanction as envisaged under the
Rules, in our opinion, the same is impermissible in
law. The appellants before the High Court have ad-mitted that the Ministry had no intention to merge the
civil and electrical streams which were two distinct
services having separate recruitment rules. The said
office orders, thus, clearly interfere with the working
of the statutory rules inasmuch as by reason thereof,
a post would be created which would be designated
as a Chief Engineer either Civil or Electrical, which
belongs to two different streams.”
In the case of Virender Singh Hooda and Ors Vs. State of Haryana
and Anr. (2004) 12 SC 588, it was held as under :
“24. The aforesaid circular is general in nature and
does not refer to any particular service or service
rules. In law if an executive instruction is contrary to
statutory rules, the rules will prevail and not the exec-utive instructions. Further reading of the aforesaid

-317-
circular shows that it is applicable to pre-existing va-cancies.”
20.In view of the above analysis of the statutory provisions and settled legal position,
this Court has no hesitation in holding that the Chief Executive Officers of the Jila
Panchayat and the Collector of the district acted beyond their jurisdiction and
authority under the Law to transfer Assistant Teachers from one Jila Panchayat to
another Jila Panchayat. All the transfer orders by which the petitioners, the
Assistant Teachers, in the above batch of petitions have been transferred from
their respective schools within the jurisdiction one Jila Panchayat to schools in the
jurisdiction of another Jila Panchayat are bad in Law and are held illegal.
21.As the impugned orders are held to be illegal due to lack of jurisdiction and
competence, I do not consider it necessary to dwell into other grounds raised by
respective petitioners to challenge the orders of transfer.
However considering that the petitioners/Assistant Teachers, large in
number, have been transferred and posted to various schools which are in the
midst of their educational session and if at this stage, the petitioners are required
to be relieved from the schools to which they have been transferred under the
impugned order, the teaching and the interest of students would be adversely
affected, it is directed that the petitioners shall continue till the end of education
session 2016-2017 followed by annual examinations. As soon as the annual
examinations are over, all the petitioners shall submit their joining back in their
respective schools where from they were transferred under the impugned orders
and their joining shall be accepted.
22.In WPS No. 1273 of 2016 and in some other cases, it has been brought to notice
of this Court that vide letter dated 16.8.2016 of the Chief Executive Officer,
Kabeerdham addressed to the Chief Executive Officer, Jila Panchayat, Raigarh, it
has been stated that in Kabeerdham district, Assistant Teachers of “Arts” are in
excess. During the course of the arguments, it was stated that Jila Panchayat,
Kabeerdham is not accepting joining of Assistant Teachers of Arts subject who
have been transferred from jurisdiction of Jila Panchayat, Raigarh to the
jurisdiction of Jila Panchayat, Kabeerdham. In respect of those
petitioners/Assistant Teachers of “Arts” whose joining are not being accepted,

-318-
those teachers will have to be permitted to join immediately if not already allowed
in their original schools where from they were transferred under the impugned
order from Jila Panchayat, Raigarh to Jila Panchayat Kabeerdham.
23. It is further made clear that this order shall not come in the way of the competent
authority in effecting rationalization and posting of petitioners/Assistant Teachers,
within the same Jila Panchayat in accordance with the rationalization policy and in
accordance with the Act of 1993, Rules of 1999 and Rules of 2012. The vacancies
of Assistant Teachers which may arise on account of Assistant Teachers joining
back to their schools of original posting may be filled-up by the competent
authority by way of direct recruitment well in advance so that before start of next
educational session, sufficient number of teachers are available to achieve the
Pupil-Teacher ratio as provided under the RTE Act and RTE Rules.
24.All the petitions are accordingly allowed in the manner and to the extent indicated
above. No orders as to costs.
Sd/-
(Manindra Mohan Shrivastava)
Judge
 Praveen
